# Fresh anonymous dispute adjudication

Read only your execution plan and its allowed reader, including every shared evidence block, completely in untruncated chunks. Confirm the sole absolute output. Tell the controller when reading is complete, then wait for authenticated release. No peer source outputs, other debates, prior assessments, calculated totals, winners, publication, biographies, network, paid calls, credentials, environment or session inspection, or subagents. Anonymous option labels must not be treated as pass identities. This is procedural isolation, not a filesystem sandbox.

Resolve only the packet’s disputes, exactly once in packet order. Choose option-a or option-b from each supplied pair. Do not average, invent a third option, change an undisputed field, change inventory structure, calculate totals or infer a winner. Select on source evidence and the frozen rubric; do not prefer compromise or one label in general. Required audio evidence must already be resolved before this context runs.

The motion is exactly “Does God exist?”. Assess William Lane Craig and Klemens Kappel under the complete two-advocate scope. Audience questions and moderator logistics are context, not the other speaker’s adopted claims. Preserve Kappel’s positive yet qualified atheistic-knowledge claim, symmetric believer/atheist example, later peer-disagreement refinement and uncertainty about a laws-and-initial-conditions scenario. Preserve Craig’s disavowal of conclusive proof, distinction between contingency and temporal beginning, causal rather than temporal priority, and generic theism versus Christianity. Both accept that knowledge needs warrant without conclusive proof or knowing that one knows. Preserve Kappel’s correction that psychological being means a being with a mind. Distinguish moral agreement from moral ontology, importance from proof of existence, historical people/reports from miracles, and confidence in a sociological explanation from knowledge of its details. Read and apply all complete source and audio records. Unresolved recognition polarity, corrupted names, incidental timing fragments and erroneous machine labels on procedural speech cannot justify a rating or a contradiction.

Output one JSON object: schemaVersion "1.0-standalone-dispute-adjudication", protocolId "assessment-production-standalone-debate-v1", status "complete-and-schema-valid", debateNumber (string) and debateId copied from packet, reviewerRole "isolated-dispute-only-adjudicator", assessmentModel "5.6 Sol", reasoningEffort "low", isolation, resolutions. isolation has passIdentitiesUnavailable, calculatedTotalsUnavailable, winnerLabelsUnavailable, publicationProseUnavailable, otherDebatesUnavailable (true only if accurate) and contaminationDetected (false only if accurate). Report any breach. resolutions exactly matches dispute order and count; each entry {disputeId,selectedOption,rationale}. selectedOption is option-a or option-b. Rationale is at least 40 characters and explains the specific source evidence and criterion supporting the choice.

After authenticated reading release, prepare the complete candidate only in memory and feed it to the pinned checker on stdin. Review all selections and rationales against source restrictions before requesting the held-candidate review. Supply the exact existing UTF-8 bytes through stdin to the pinned maintained runner’s handoff hold command with explicit debate and plan; keep its process alive and report the receipt. Do not create draft candidate files. The controller reads the whole candidate, verifies source compliance and releases its exact one-time save. One context, one submission, zero automatic retries; no overwrites or post-submission authoring.

According to Linets, the most fundamental question of philosophy is why is there something rather than nothing? Even if the universe is eternal in the past, we could still ask why there is an eternal universe instead of no universe at all. I can't think of any contemporary philosopher or physicist who thinks that the universe exists necessarily. For it's easy to conceive of a possible world in which a different collection of fundamental particles or fundamental fields exists or even no particles or no fields at all. The universe does not therefore exist necessarily but contingently. But if the universe exists contingently, then the obvious question arises. Why? Why is this contingency realized when it doesn't have to exist? The explanation of why the universe exists can be found only in a transcendent reality which exists by a necessity of its own nature. Now, there are only two possible candidates that could fit such a description. Either an abstract object like a number or else an unembodied mind or consciousness. But abstract objects don't stand in causal relations. The number seven, for example, even if it exists, has no effect on anything. It follows logically that the explanation of the existence of the universe is an unembodied mind which is what theists take God to be. If you ask why does God exist? The answer is that God like numbers, sets and other mathematical objects exists by a necessity of his own nature if he exists. That cannot be said of the contingent universe. We can summarize liinets's argument as follows. One, every contingent entity has an explanation of its existence. Two, if the universe has an explanation of its existence, that explanation is God. Three, the universe is a contingent entity. Four, therefore, the universe has an explanation of its existence. Five. Therefore, the explanation of the existence of the universe is God. Now, I'm not claiming that Livinets's argument is a knockdown proof of God's existence. But I do think that each of its three premises is more plausibly true than not. And therefore, this gives us good evidence for God's existence. As professor cppel has written by evidence I mean observations or theoretical considerations that point us in the direction of the existence of God. Evidence does not have to be conclusive.         "excerpt": "finetuning of the universe. In recent decades, scientists have been stunned by the discovery that the initial conditions of the Big Bang were fine-tuned for the existence of intelligent life with a precision and delicacy that literally defy human comprehension. This fine-tuning is of two sorts. First, when the laws of nature are expressed as mathematical equations, you find appearing in them certain constants like the gravitational constant. These constants are not determined by the laws of nature. Second, in addition to these constants, there are certain arbitrary quantities which are just put in as initial conditions on which the laws of nature operate. for example, the amount of entropy in the universe. Now, all of these constants and quantities fall into an extraordinarily narrow range of life permitting values. Were these constants or quantities to be altered by less than a hair's breath, the life permitting balance would be destroyed and life would not exist. We now know that lifep prohibiting universes are incomprehensibly more probable than any lifepermitting universe. Now there are three live explanatory options for this extraordinary fine-tuning. Physical necessity, chance or design. Physical necessity is not however a plausible explanation because as we've seen the constants and quantities are independent of the laws of nature. Therefore they are not physically necessary. So maybe the fine-tuning is due to chance. Well, the problem with this explanation is that the odds of a life permitting universe governed by our laws of nature are just so infinite decimal that they cannot be reasonably faced. Therefore, in order to rescue the hypothesis of chance, theorists have been forced to postulate the existence of a world ensemble of other universes, preferably infinite in number and randomly ordered, so that life permitting universes would appear by chance somewhere in the ensemble. Not only is this hypothesis, to borrow Richard Dawkins phrase, an unparimmonious extravagance, but it also faces an insuperable objection. By far, most of the observable universes in a world ensemble would be worlds in which a single brain fluctuates into existence out of the vacuum and observes its world. Thus, if our world were just a random member of a world ensemble, we ought to be having observations like that. Since we don't, that strongly disisconfirms the hypothesis of the existence of a world ensemble. So, chance is not a good explanation. Design then seems to be the best explanation of the finetuning of the universe. There is an intelligent mind behind the cosmos which designed it to be life permitting. Hence, we may formulate the following inductive argument. One, the universe is fine-tuned for intelligent interactive life. Two, the finetuning is due to either physical necessity, chance, or design. Three, the best explanation is design. For therefore, a transcendent designer of the universe exists. Thus, the fine-tuning of the universe constitutes evidence for God. Another example of a theistic"

        "excerpt": "based on the fact of the origin of the universe. We now have pretty strong evidence that the universe is not eternal in the past but had an absolute beginning a finite time ago. In 2003, Arvin Board, Alan Guth, and Alexander Venin were able to prove that any universe which is on average in a state of cosmic expansion throughout its history cannot be infinite in the past but must have a past space-time boundary. What makes their proof so powerful is that it holds regardless of the physical description of the early universe. Because we don't yet have a quantum theory of gravity, we can't yet provide a physical description of the first split second of the existence of the universe. But the Borg Guth Felankan theorem is independent of any physical description of that moment. Their theorem implies that the quantum vacuum state of the early universe, which some scientific popularizations have misleadingly and inaccurately referred to as nothing, cannot be eternal in the past, but must have had an absolute beginning. Even if our universe is just a tiny part of a so-called multiverse composed of many universes, their theorem requires that the multiverse itself must have a beginning. Of course, highly speculative scenarios such as loop quantum gravity models, string models, even closed timelike curves have been proposed to try to avoid this absolute beginning. These models are fraught with problems. But the bottom line is that none of these theories, even if true, succeed in restoring an eternal past. At most, they just push the beginning back a step. At a conference earlier this year on the occasion of Stephven Hawkings 70th birthday, Vilin summed up the situation with these words and I quote, \"All the evidence we have says that the universe had a beginning.\" End quote. Now, it's difficult to see how there can be any explanation of this fact apart from theism. The only alternative to theism seems to be that the universe just came into being uncaused from nothing. But that just is to admit that there is no explanation of the origin of the universe. There just doesn't seem to be any competing hypothesis to theism available to the atheist to explain the origin of the universe. Hence, one may present the following inference. One, the universe had an absolute beginning in the past. Two, the best explanation of the absolute beginning of the universe is a transcendent creator. Three, therefore, a transcendent creator exists. So once more, we have evidence for God in the form of an inference to the best explanation."

DOCUMENT docs/reassessment-rubric-v2.1.md
# Slugfester Reassessment Rubric v2.1

Score the argumentative performance present in the locked source—not whether a worldview is true, admirable, popular, or familiar. A score is earned move by move and has no automatic floor.

## Shared band anchors

| Band | Operational meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 95–100 | Exceptional and unusually complete: explicit inferential structure, strong public warrant, direct engagement with the strongest live reply, and little material repair needed. |
| 85–94 | Very strong: the move substantially advances its burden and survives the best live objection, with only bounded residual gaps. |
| 75–84 | Strong/competent: coherent and relevant, but compressed, incompletely warranted, or vulnerable at one important bridge. |
| 65–74 | Mixed: a useful core is present, yet a material premise, inference, or reply remains underdeveloped. |
| 50–64 | Weak: a major burden is not discharged, or the move largely misses the live issue despite some relevant content. |
| 25–49 | Very weak: the inference is seriously defective, substantially unsupported, or mostly unresponsive. |
| 0–24 | Non-performance: no usable argument on the assigned issue, a self-defeating contradiction, or content that is essentially irrelevant to the burden. |

Use 50, 70, 80, and 90 as decision boundaries, not default magnets. Before moving a dimension by five points, identify the particular transcript evidence or missing bridge that warrants the change.

## Dimensions

### Logical coherence — 25%

- **90+**: premises, intermediate steps, scope, and conclusion are explicit and mutually consistent; the strongest live validity challenge is answered.
- **75–89**: the inference is intelligible and mostly valid but leaves one bridge implicit or one ambiguity unresolved.
- **50–74**: the conclusion depends on a material equivocation, unsupported conditional, false dilemma, internal tension, or non sequitur.
- **Below 50**: the move is self-undermining, plainly invalid at its core, or lacks an inferential route to its conclusion.

Do not lower this dimension merely because a premise lacks evidence; that belongs primarily under evidence/warrant.

### Evidence and warrant — 20%

- **90+**: relevant evidence is specific, sourceable, representative, and connected to the conclusion through defended warrants and serious alternative comparison.
- **75–89**: appropriate support is offered, but documentation, independence, base rates, rival hypotheses, or a key warrant remains compressed.
- **50–74**: authority, anecdote, assertion, selective examples, or speculative mechanisms carry substantial weight without adequate checking.
- **Below 50**: a fact-dependent conclusion is effectively unsupported or relies on evidence contradicted within the locked record.

Lack of an inline citation is not itself disproof. Score the degree to which verification matters to the inference.

### Responsiveness — 20%

- **90+**: accurately identifies and answers the opponent's strongest relevant claim at its decisive premise or inference.
- **75–89**: directly engages the live issue but leaves a major component unanswered or answers it only conditionally.
- **50–74**: responds to a secondary point, a weaker reconstruction, or only one part of a compound objection.
- **Below 50**: evades, changes the subject, or attacks a position the opponent did not advance.

Constructive openings are scored for responsiveness to the motion and known burden, not penalized for lacking an earlier opponent move.

### Relevance and burden — 15%

- **90+**: directly discharges a locked burden or decisively defeats an opponent burden, with a conclusion proportionate to what that success establishes.
- **75–89**: materially advances the burden but does not complete it or leaves one required link unstated.
- **50–74**: related to the topic but peripheral, burden-shifting, or stronger in rhetoric than in motion-level consequence.
- **Below 50**: irrelevant to the locked motion or depends on assigning the opponent a burden they did not adopt.

Judge opportunity fairly. Do not invent a positive contrary burden for a side whose declared role is critical.

### Precision and clarity — 10%

- **90+**: key terms, modality, comparison class, scope, and confidence are stable and easy to audit.
- **75–89**: the move is understandable with only minor ambiguity or compression.
- **50–74**: unstable terminology, vague quantifiers, unclear referents, or missing distinctions materially obscure evaluation.
- **Below 50**: the claim cannot be reconstructed reliably enough to assess.

Fluency, wit, and polish are not clarity unless they improve the recoverable argument.

### Calibration and charity — 10%

- **90+**: confidence matches the evidence, concessions are accurate, alternatives are represented in their strongest live form, and uncertainty is explicit.
- **75–89**: generally proportionate and fair, with limited overstatement or incomplete treatment of an alternative.
- **50–74**: significant overclaiming, selective skepticism, motive attribution, or an avoidably weak representation of the alternative.
- **Below 50**: certainty is radically disproportionate or the move depends on a gross caricature.

Do not award charity merely for politeness or deduct it for an abrasive tone unless the tone changes the argumentative representation.

## Penalty controls

- Identify one primary home for each defect. Apply effects in additional dimensions only when the transcript shows genuinely distinct consequences.
- Compatibility is not explanation; possibility can answer contradiction without establishing probability.
- Familiar named arguments receive no pedigree bonus. Score only the version actually presented.
- A fallacy or bias tag never creates an extra deduction.
- Audience response, status, accent, speed, and confidence are excluded.
- Missing replies are represented by response links and burden completion; do not manufacture paired moves.
- Score diagnostic questions as questions or challenges, not as unargued contrary theories.

## Aggregation

Move scores use fixed dimension weights. Section scores are rounded importance-weighted means of selected moves. Overall scores are prelocked section-weighted means plus a −5 to +5 burden-completion adjustment. No additional coverage, coherence, resilience, or global-calibration meta-score is added; those qualities are already visible in the moves and burden record.

See [`assessment-workflow-v2.1.md`](assessment-workflow-v2.1.md) for formulas, disagreement thresholds, provenance, and promotion gates.

END DOCUMENT

E0170 [542080..549440] could fit such a description. Either an
E0171 [545440..553200] abstract object like a number or else an
E0172 [549440..556640] unembodied mind or consciousness. But
E0173 [553200..558240] abstract objects don't stand in causal
E0174 [556640..561279] relations. The number seven, for
E0175 [558240..564399] example, even if it exists, has no
E0176 [561279..566640] effect on anything. It follows logically
E0177 [564399..570480] that the explanation of the existence of
E0178 [566640..574800] the universe is an unembodied mind which
E0179 [570480..577920] is what theists take God to be. If you
E0180 [574800..581040] ask why does God exist? The answer is
E0181 [577920..583760] that God like numbers, sets and other
E0182 [581040..586279] mathematical objects exists by a
E0183 [583760..589279] necessity of his own nature if he
E0184 [586279..592320] exists. That cannot be said of the
E0185 [589279..595760] contingent universe. We can summarize
E0186 [592320..598399] liinets's argument as follows. One,
E0187 [595760..602000] every contingent entity has an
E0188 [598399..604480] explanation of its existence. Two, if
E0189 [602000..608080] the universe has an explanation of its
E0190 [604480..611640] existence, that explanation is God.
E0191 [608080..614240] Three, the universe is a contingent
E0192 [611640..617120] entity. Four, therefore, the universe
E0193 [614240..620240] has an explanation of its existence.
E0194 [617120..622680] Five. Therefore, the explanation of the
E0195 [620240..625120] existence of the universe is
E0196 [622680..627920] God. Now, I'm not claiming that
E0197 [625120..630800] Livinets's argument is a knockdown proof
E0198 [627920..633680] of God's existence. But I do think that
E0199 [630800..636560] each of its three premises is more
E0200 [633680..639040] plausibly true than not. And therefore,
E0201 [636560..641600] this gives us good evidence for God's
E0202 [639040..645440] existence. As professor cppel has
E0203 [641600..648800] written by evidence I mean observations
E0204 [645440..651519] or theoretical considerations that point
E0205 [648800..654920] us in the direction of the existence of
E0206 [651519..658399] God. Evidence does not have to be
E0207 [654920..661680] conclusive. End quote. Linen's argument
E0208 [658399..664000] certainly rises to that standard. There
E0209 [661680..666320] are also other philosophical arguments
E0210 [664000..668920] that are worth discussing here such as
E0211 [666320..671360] the moral argument and the ontological
E0212 [668920..674079] argument. But in the interests of time,
E0213 [671360..677800] let's press on to the second sort of
E0214 [674079..680160] evidence, inference to the best
E0215 [677800..682399] explanation. In inference to the best
E0216 [680160..685360] explanation, we begin with a body of
E0217 [682399..688880] facts to be explained. We then assemble
E0218 [685360..690880] a pool of live explanatory options to
E0219 [688880..694480] account for these facts.
E0220 [690880..698399] Finally, we select that explanation
E0221 [694480..701519] which if true would best explain the
E0222 [698399..703720] facts in question. An excellent example
E0223 [701519..706160] of a theistic inference to the best
E0224 [703720..709360] explanation is the argument from the
E0225 [706160..711760] finetuning of the universe. In recent
E0226 [709360..713920] decades, scientists have been stunned by
E0227 [711760..716000] the discovery that the initial
E0228 [713920..718079] conditions of the Big Bang were
E0229 [716000..721040] fine-tuned for the existence of
E0230 [718079..725079] intelligent life with a precision and
E0231 [721040..727680] delicacy that literally defy human
E0232 [725079..731200] comprehension. This fine-tuning is of
E0233 [727680..733600] two sorts. First, when the laws of
E0234 [731200..736079] nature are expressed as mathematical
E0235 [733600..739160] equations, you find appearing in them
E0236 [736079..742079] certain constants like the gravitational
E0237 [739160..745519] constant. These constants are not
E0238 [742079..748320] determined by the laws of nature.
E0239 [745519..750959] Second, in addition to these constants,
E0240 [748320..753519] there are certain arbitrary quantities
E0241 [750959..756160] which are just put in as initial
E0242 [753519..758880] conditions on which the laws of nature
E0243 [756160..762320] operate. for example, the amount of
E0244 [758880..765120] entropy in the universe. Now, all of

E0258 [802519..808079] design. Physical necessity is not
E0259 [805279..810320] however a plausible explanation because
E0260 [808079..812720] as we've seen the constants and
E0261 [810320..815120] quantities are independent of the laws
E0262 [812720..816600] of nature. Therefore they are not
E0263 [815120..819680] physically
E0264 [816600..822720] necessary. So maybe the fine-tuning is
E0265 [819680..825519] due to chance. Well, the problem with
E0266 [822720..827839] this explanation is that the odds of a
E0267 [825519..831200] life permitting universe governed by our
E0268 [827839..834320] laws of nature are just so infinite
E0269 [831200..837600] decimal that they cannot be reasonably
E0270 [834320..840560] faced. Therefore, in order to rescue the

E0278 [860560..866440] Not only is this hypothesis, to borrow

DOCUMENT docs/assessment-production/standalone-debates-v1/debate-284/source/assessment-source-notes.md
# Source restrictions and complete-recording review

The exact motion is “Does God exist?”. William Lane Craig is pro (“God exists”); Klemens Kappel is con (“God does not exist”). Kappel explicitly defends rational atheism and the stronger claim that appropriately situated people can know that God does not exist. Do not reduce his adopted position to mere withholding of belief. Equally, do not invent a demand for conclusive proof: both speakers repeatedly distinguish justification from certainty. Score only this recording, not external reputations, later views, or doctrinal truth.

## Coverage and speakers

The full 148:17 upload has one English automatic-caption track. Both openings, two rounds of rebuttals, both closings and all audience answers are present. Formal floor transfers: Craig starts 4:40; Kappel starts 24:43 (substance from 26:00); Craig 45:26; Kappel 57:31; Craig 1:10:03; Kappel 1:18:33; Craig closing 1:26:17; Kappel closing 1:31:34; audience questions start 1:36:52. Closing thanks begin 2:27:28. The sole eleven-second caption gap is the closing-to-closing handoff, not missing substantive speech. Caption event durations overlap; the last Craig event ends slightly after the moderator begins. Do not infer shared speech from overlap alone.

The unnamed moderator introduces speakers, keeps time, allocates questions and permits an extra minute at 2:10:03. He supplies no independent motion argument. Audience prompts, their assertions and follow-up objections remain unscored context. The speakers’ replies remain eligible. A questioner's premises do not automatically become the other debater's commitments. In particular, distinguish Quran-science claims (1:36:52), the audience's cosmological criticism (1:41:37), electron anti-realism prompt (1:45:39), ancient parallel claims (1:49:42), modal-ontological prompt (1:54:01), contingent-universe question (1:58:42), abstract-laws/calculator question (2:06:57), importance challenges (2:11:26 and 2:16:15), and hostile-Jewish-witness/Caesar question (2:22:53). Several prompts have argumentative follow-ups; neither named opponent receives credit for those words. Kappel or Craig can adopt a proposition in their own reply, but it must be evidenced there.

## Preserve qualifications and argumentative targets

Craig offers contingency, fine-tuning, cosmic beginning and resurrection in his opening; adds morality and consciousness in his first rebuttal; and later personal experience. His initial contingency route does not depend on a temporal beginning. Do not merge it indiscriminately with the separate origin argument. His cosmology distinguishes a quantum vacuum from literal nothing and later causal priority from temporal priority. Preserve those qualifications while evaluating the bridges he actually supplies.

Kappel presents a largely naturalistic/empiricist background and an explanation of reasonable atheism. He declines an attempt at a cogent argument on premises a firm theist must accept; this is narrower than conceding there can be no warrant for atheism. His Adam/Bertrand example is explicitly symmetric, and he allows the believer could be right. At 40:07 he acknowledges question-begging relative to a theist who rejects his framework; preserve this dialectical qualification. He distinguishes psychological sources of belief from its evidential assessment, and separates faith's practical value from the truth of its assumption.

At 1:00:36 Kappel clarifies that “psychological being” means a being with a mind, not a being created by human thought. Do not perpetuate Craig's first-rebuttal reading after this correction. The magical-star examples illustrate his standards of hypothesis evaluation; don't turn the parody into Kappel's asserted astronomy. Craig's physical/transcendent distinction is his answer to those analogies.

Kappel's first rebuttal explicitly says knowledge does not require conclusive evidence, eliminating all alternatives, or knowing that one knows. His climate analogy concerns this epistemic point. Craig subsequently agrees that knowledge need not be conclusive or higher-order knowledge, but asks what warrant supports atheism. Both the agreement and the remaining dispute matter. Kappel's claim about unequal footing refers specifically to a prior debate opponent and explicitly qualifies “maybe not this debate”; don't assign that person's science denial to Craig.

Kappel's first rebuttal challenges contested moral and ontological premises, reliability of ordinary causal intuitions at the universe's origin, and the inference from a different-order cause to a prayer-hearing moral mind. His second rebuttal distinguishes alleged scientific explanation by laws/initial conditions from an origin at which those laws begin, presenting the scenario conditionally and admitting uncertainty. Do not render this as established physics or a dogmatic claim that every event is causeless. Craig's closing gives personal explanation as the alternative; their later mind/body discussion materially revisits that answer.

On disagreement, preserve chronological development: Kappel first infers uncertainty from philosophical controversy; Craig denies that controversy means personal uncertainty; Kappel's closing articulates peer-disagreement as a reason to reduce confidence and acknowledges the higher-order disagreement about that very theory. It would be inaccurate to omit either his earlier phrasing or later refinement.

Kappel distinguishes agreement that child abuse is wrong from metaphysical agreement about objective moral properties, and cites alternative moral theories. Craig appeals to moral experience and intuition, contrasts moral nihilism, and later argues that God's existence changes meaning/purpose/value. These are distinct burdens: truth of theism, metaphysical grounding, practical importance, and ultimate meaning. Do not automatically classify discussion of importance as an attempted proof from beneficial consequences. Kappel's late comments mention meaningful moral questions without offering a fully developed rival ethical theory. Preserve this scope.

During questions, Kappel distinguishes the existence of historical Jesus and Caesar from reports of miracles and divinity. At 2:25:43 he expresses confidence in naturalistic sociological explanation while conceding ignorance of its precise details. Craig appeals to the historical setting and then his three resurrection facts. Do not omit those different levels of claim, treat historical existence as conceded divinity, or invent a denial that the reports exist. On religious diversity, assess the speaker's own comparative inference, not the audience's Islam defense. Craig concedes that initial metaphysical arguments do not alone establish Christianity.

## Recognition uncertainty and audio checks

Keep exact caption text and timestamps. Names are repeatedly garbled: Kappel, Leibniz, Borde–Guth–Vilenkin, Thor, phlogiston, Kant and others. These spellings are transcription limitations, not speaker errors. Do not use corrupted names as quotations. A technical term can be identified from unambiguous surrounding context in a paraphrase, but do not silently edit the source.

Several caption negations/pronouns are suspect: Craig around 46:46–47:19 alternates “exist”/“does not exist” in reporting Bertrand; Kappel at 1:01:25–1:01:39 has an apparent polarity reversal; Craig at 1:12:02–1:12:09 is captioned as saying his arguments “were knockdown arguments” despite explicitly disavowing that earlier and later. These are mandatory wording checks if a selected move or evaluation relies on them. Don't score them as contradictions before verification. Kappel's “six billion years” at 2:12:44 is incidental to his moral-implication question and should not become a deduction based on uncertain recognition.

Question exchanges contain multiple speakers in single events: 1:37:39, 1:38:45, 1:40:34, 1:44:37, 1:48:37, 1:55:05, 1:56:27–1:57:22, 2:01:53–2:02:29, 2:04:50–2:05:39, 2:08:59, 2:10:03, 2:13:39–2:14:02, 2:14:56, 2:19:06–2:21:17 and 2:25:00–2:26:24. Selected moves depending on these boundaries require explicit ownership and below-high confidence where attribution is materially uncertain. Do not select broad multi-speaker blocks as one person's words. Purely logistical fragments can remain context. Mark genuine material uncertainty honestly; no forced-high attribution to avoid an audio check.

No paid transcription or direct listening has occurred at this freeze. Targeted machine audio verification will cross-check central wording and material speaker boundaries before primary judgments. Its result must be added as an explicit supplemental source record, never a silent change to canonical captions. All required below-high inventory moves must receive a matching completed verification record before adjudication. A successful machine transcript is not direct listening and is not an external fact-check of philosophical or historical claims.

END DOCUMENT
